## Q76

Biology · botany · Microbes in Human Welfare

Match the following list of microbes and their importance:

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Sacharomyces cerevisiae | (i) | Production of immunosuppressive agents |
| (b) | Monascus purpureus | (ii) | Ripening of Swiss cheese |
| (c) | Trichoderma polysporum | (iii) | Commercial production of ethanol |
| (d) | Propionibacterium shermanii | (iv) | Production of blood-cholesterol lowering agents |

- (1) (a)-(iii), (b)-(i), (c)-(iv), (d)-(ii)
- (2) (a)-(iii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(ii)
- (3) (a)-(iv), (b)-(iii), (c)-(ii), (d)-(i)
- (4) (a)-(iv), (b)-(ii), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii)

**Answer** — booklet: (none) · independent solve: (2) · unchecked

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\textit{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}$ (brewer's yeast) is used for commercial ethanol production, so (a)-(iii).
2. $\textit{Monascus purpureus}$ yields statins, blood-cholesterol lowering agents, so (b)-(iv).
3. $\textit{Trichoderma polysporum}$ yields cyclosporin A, an immunosuppressive agent, so (c)-(i).
4. $\textit{Propionibacterium shermanii}$ produces CO$_2$ that forms the holes in Swiss cheese during ripening, so (d)-(ii).
5. The match (a)-(iii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(ii) is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Chemicals, Enzymes and Other Bioactive Molecules, Microbes in Industrial Products (Fermented Beverages) · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

## Q77

Biology · both · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Arrange the following events of meiosis in correct sequence

- **((a))** Crossing over
- **((b))** Synapsis
- **((c))** Terminalisation of chaismata
- **((d))** Disappearance of nucleolus

- (1) (b), (c), (d), (a)
- (2) (b), (a), (d), (c)
- **(3)** (b), (a), (c), (d)
- (4) (a), (b), (c), (d)

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The sequence of event during meiosis are
2. (1) Synapsis (Zygotene)
3. (2) Crossing over (Pachytene)
4. (3) Terminalisation of chiasmata
5. (4) Disappearance of nucleolus

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Synapsis, the pairing of homologous chromosomes, takes place in zygotene, so $(b)$ comes first.
2. Crossing over takes place between paired homologues in pachytene, so $(a)$ comes second.
3. Chiasmata terminalise during diakinesis, so $(c)$ comes third.
4. The nucleolus disappears at the end of diakinesis, just before metaphase I, so $(d)$ comes last.
5. The order $(b) \rightarrow (a) \rightarrow (c) \rightarrow (d)$ is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Prophase I and Its Types · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 23)

![printed question 77](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q077_88328986_28102026.png?v=88328986e2f2)

## Q86

Biology · zoology · Locomotion and Movement

Which of the following is not a function of the skeletal system?

- (2) Production of erythrocytes
- (3) Storage of minerals
- **(4)** Production of body heat

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The skeletal system provides support and acts as levers for $\text{locomotion}$ (option $1$) together with muscles.
2. Red bone marrow carries out haemopoiesis, producing erythrocytes (option $2$).
3. Bone matrix stores minerals such as $\text{Ca}^{2+}$ and phosphate (option $3$).
4. Body heat is produced mainly by the metabolic activity and contraction of skeletal muscles, not by bones, so option $4$ is not a skeletal function.

## Q89

Biology · botany · Biological Classification

Select the wrong statement:

- (1) Mosaic disease in tobacco and AIDS in human being are caused by viruses
- **(2)** The viroids were discovered by D.J. Ivanowski
- (3) W.M. Stanley showed that viruses could be crystallized
- (4) The term 'contagium vivum fluidum' was coined by M.W. Beijerinek

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The viroids were discovered by T.O. Diener.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Tobacco mosaic disease (TMV) and AIDS (HIV) are both viral diseases, so option $1$ is correct.
2. Viroids were discovered by T.O. Diener in $1971$; Ivanowski ($1892$) only showed the tobacco mosaic agent passes bacteria-proof filters, so option $2$ is wrong.
3. W.M. Stanley ($1935$) showed viruses could be crystallised, so option $3$ is correct.
4. M.W. Beijerinck ($1898$) called the infectious fluid 'contagium vivum fluidum', so option $4$ is correct.
5. The wrong statement is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Viroids, Prions & Lichens · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 26)

## Q91

Biology · zoology · Excretory Products and Their Elimination

Human urine is usually acidic because:

- **(1)** Hydrogen ions are actively secreted into the filtrate.
- (2) The sodium transporter exchanges one hydrogen ion for each sodium ion, in peritubular capillaries.
- (3) Excreted plasma proteins are acidic
- (4) Potassium and sodium exchange generates acidity

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Tubular secretion maintains the pH and ionic balance of body fluids in which hydrogen ions are actively secreted into the filterate and bicarbonate ions are reabsorbed.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The PCT and DCT actively secrete $\mathrm{H^+}$ into the filtrate (tubular secretion) to keep the body's acid-base balance; this added $\mathrm{H^+}$ makes urine slightly acidic ($\mathrm{pH} \approx 6$), which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Function of the Tubules and their types · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 26)

Biology · botany · Plant Growth and Development

Auxin can be bioassayed by :

- (1) Lettuce hypocotyl elongation
- **(2)** Avena coleoptile curvature
- (3) Hydroponics
- (4) Potometer

## Q93

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

Which of the following events is not associated with ovulation in human female?

- (1) LH surge
- **(2)** Decrease in estradiol
- (3) Full development of Graafian follicle
- (4) Release of secondary oocyte

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In $28$ days reproductive cycle, ovulation occurs on $14$th day due to LH surge.
2. In the mid cycle, the level of FSH and estrogen are also high.
3. The female gamete is released from the ovary in secondary oocyte stage after completing meiosis I.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. At mid-cycle the fully developed Graafian follicle secretes rising estrogen (estradiol), which triggers the LH surge; the LH surge causes rupture of the Graafian follicle and release of the secondary oocyte, so options $1$, $3$ and $4$ are associated with ovulation while a decrease in estradiol is not, giving option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Menstrual Cycle · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 27)

![printed question 93](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q093_fb87ab81_28102026.png?v=fb87ab812842)

## Q94

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

Body having meshwork of cells, internal cavities lined with food filtering flagellated cells and indirect development are the characteristics of phylum:

- (1) Protozoa
- (2) Coelenterata
- **(3)** Porifera
- (4) Mollusca

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In poriferans, the body is loose aggregate of cells (meshwork of cells).
2. Internal cavities and canals are lined with food filtering flagellated cells i.e. choanocyte/collar cell.
3. Choanocytes help in filter feeding.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Cellular-level body with a water canal system and spongocoel lined by food-filtering flagellated choanocytes (collar cells), with indirect development through a larva, defines phylum Porifera, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Phylum – Porifera · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q95

Biology · zoology · Chemical Coordination and Integration

Which one of the following hormones is not involved in sugar metabolism?

- (1) Glucagon
- (2) Cortisone
- **(3)** Aldosterone
- (4) Insulin

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Mineralocorticoid (Aldosterone) has no role in sugar metabolism.
2. It helps in salt metabolism.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Glucagon raises and insulin lowers blood glucose, and cortisone (a glucocorticoid) promotes gluconeogenesis, but aldosterone (a mineralocorticoid) controls only $Na^+$, $K^+$ and water balance, so the answer is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Adrenal Gland · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 27)

## Q96

Biology · zoology · Human Health and Disease

Which of the following diseases is caused by a protozoan?

- (1) Blastomycosis
- (2) Syphilis
- (3) Influenza
- **(4)** Babesiosis

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Babesiosis is a disease caused by a protozoan, Babesia bigemina.
2. The vector is tick, so disease is also called tick fever in cattle.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Blastomycosis is a fungal disease (Blastomyces), syphilis is bacterial (Treponema pallidum) and influenza is viral, while babesiosis is caused by the protozoan Babesia, so the answer is option $4$.

## Q97

Biology · both · Strategies for Enhancement in Food Production

Outbreeding is an important strategy of animal husbandry because it :

- (1) Exposes harmful recessive genes that are eliminated by selection
- (2) Helps in accumulation of superior genes.
- (3) Is useful in producing purelines of animals.
- **(4)** Is useful in overcoming inbreeding depression

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. A single outcross (a type of outbreeding) is useful in overcoming inbreeding depression.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Exposing harmful recessives, accumulating superior genes and producing purelines are all advantages of inbreeding as NCERT teaches it; outbreeding, i.e. mating unrelated animals, is used to overcome inbreeding depression, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Animal Breeding · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q99

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

A jawless fish, which lays eggs in fresh water and whose ammocoetes larvae after metamorphosis return to the ocean is:

- **(1)** Petromyzon
- (2) Eptatretus
- (3) Myxine
- (4) Neomyxine

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Petromyzon (Lamprey) is a migratory marine water jawless fish which shows anadromous migration.
2. It spawns in fresh water, stops feeding and dies.
3. Its larva (Ammocoetes) after metamorphosis will return to ocean.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. NCERT: cyclostomes such as $Petromyzon$ (lamprey) are marine but migrate to fresh water for spawning, and after a few days their larvae metamorphose and return to the ocean; the ammocoete larva belongs to lampreys, whereas $Myxine$, $Eptatretus$ and $Neomyxine$ are marine hagfishes without a larval stage, so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Class – Cyclostomata, Chondrichthyes and Osteichthyes · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 28)

## Q101

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

If you suspect major deficiency of antibodies in a person, to which of the following would you look for confirmatory evidence?

- **(1)** Serum globulins
- (2) Fibrinogin in plasma
- (3) Serum albumins
- (4) Haemocytes

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Antibodies are present in serum.
2. They are glycoproteins and also called gammaglobulins synthesized in lymph nodes.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Antibodies are immunoglobulins, which make up the $\gamma$-globulin fraction of serum proteins, so confirmatory evidence of antibody deficiency is a low level of serum globulins, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Blood and Plasma · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q102

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

In human females, meiosis-II is not completed until?

- (1) Birth
- (2) Puberty
- **(3)** Fertilization
- (4) Uterine implantation

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In human females, meiosis II is completed after the entry of sperm into the cytoplasm of secondary oocyte at the time of fertilisation leading to the formation of ovum and IInd polar body.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The secondary oocyte released at ovulation stays arrested in meiosis-II; sperm entry at fertilization induces completion of meiosis-II, giving the ovum (ootid) and the second polar body, so the answer is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Female Gametogenesis (Oogenesis) · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 28)

## Q106

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

In angiosperms, microsporogenesis and megasporogenesis :

- (1) Occur in ovule
- (2) Occur in anther
- (3) Form gametes without furthers divisions
- **(4)** Involve meiosis

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In angiosperms, microsporogenesis and megasporogenesis involve meiosis

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Microsporogenesis occurs in the anther, megasporogenesis in the ovule, so options $1$ and $2$ each describe only one process.
2. Spores formed undergo further mitotic divisions to form the gametophytes that produce gametes, so option $3$ is false.
3. Both processes form haploid ($n$) spores from diploid ($2n$) mother cells by meiosis, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Microsporangium and Microsporogenesis, Megasporogenesis · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 29)

## Q110

The wings of a bird and the wings of an insect are :

- (1) Homologous structures and represent convergent evolution
- (2) Homologous structures and represent divergent evolution
- **(3)** Analogous structures and represent convergent evolution
- (4) Phylogenetic structures and represent divergent evolution

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The wings of a bird and an insect are analogous structure which differ in structure and origin but perform similar functions and represent convergent evolution.

1. Bird and insect wings both work for flight but are built differently and come from different origins, so they are analogous structures that show convergent evolution (option $3$).

Biology · botany · Environmental Issues

Increase in concentration of the toxicant at successive trophic levels is known as :

- (1) Biogeochemical cycling
- **(2)** Biomagnification
- (3) Biodeterioration
- (4) Biotransformation

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Increase in concentration of toxic substances in successive trophic level.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. A toxicant that accumulates in organisms and cannot be metabolised or excreted reaches higher concentrations at each successive trophic level; this is called biomagnification, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Water Pollution and Its Control · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q113

Biology · zoology · Neural Control and Coordination

Destruction of the anterior horn cells of the spinal cord would result in loss of :-

- (1) Integrating impulses
- (2) Sensory impulses
- **(3)** Voluntary motor impulses
- (4) Commissural impulses

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Anterior horn cells are ventral horn cells of spinal cord which consists of motor neurons.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The anterior (ventral) horn of the spinal cord grey matter contains the cell bodies of motor neurons whose axons leave via the ventral root to skeletal muscles; their destruction abolishes voluntary motor impulses, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Human Neural System and Its Divisions · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q114

Roots play insignificant role in absorption of water in :

- **(3)** Pistia
- (4) Pea

## Q116

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Identify the correct order of organisation of genetic material from largest to smallest :

- (1) Chromosome, genome, nucleotide, gene
- (2) Chromosome, gene, genome, nucleotide
- (3) Genome, chromosomes, nucleotide, gene
- **(4)** Genome, chromosome, gene, nucleotide

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Order of organisation of genetic material

![printed figure: solution_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q116_sol_1_e01e0ab1_28102026.png?v=e01e0ab145ea)


**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The genome is the whole genetic material, made up of all the chromosomes, so $\text{genome} > \text{chromosome}$; each chromosome carries many genes, so $\text{chromosome} > \text{gene}$; a gene is a DNA segment of many nucleotides, so $\text{gene} > \text{nucleotide}$. This gives option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Introduction to Molecular Basis of Inheritance · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 31)

## Q117

Biology · zoology · Chemical Coordination and Integration

- (3) Luteinizing hormone
- (4) Prolactin

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

## Q118

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

Read the different components from (a) to (d) in the list given below and tell the correct order of the components with reference to their arrangement from outer side to inner side in a woody dicot stem:
The correct order is :

- **((a))** Secondary cortex
- **((b))** Wood
- **((c))** Secondary phloem
- **((d))** Phellem

- (1) (d), (c), (a), (b)
- (2) (c), (d), (b), (a)
- (3) (a), (b), (d), (c)
- **(4)** (d), (a), (c), (b)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Sequence of different components of woody dicot stem from outerside to inner side is:

![printed figure: solution_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q118_sol_1_b4b10af6_28102026.png?v=b4b10af6f2c5)


**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Cork cambium (phellogen) cuts off phellem (cork) to the outside, so $(d)$ phellem is outermost.
2. Phellogen cuts off phelloderm (secondary cortex) to the inside, so $(a)$ secondary cortex comes next.
3. Vascular cambium cuts off secondary phloem to the outside, so $(c)$ secondary phloem lies inside the secondary cortex.
4. Vascular cambium cuts off secondary xylem (wood) to the inside, so $(b)$ wood is innermost.
5. Order $(d) \to (a) \to (c) \to (b)$ matches option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Secondary Growth · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 31)

![printed question 118](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q118_105b5608_28102026.png?v=105b56089312)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

## Q120

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Which one of the following is not applicable to RNA?

- **(1)** Chargaff's rule
- (2) Complementary base pairing
- (3) 5' phosphoryl and 3' hydroxyl ends
- (4) Heterocyclic nitrogenous bases

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Chargaff's rule is applicable only for DNA.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Chargaff's rule ($A = T$, $G = C$, $\frac{A+G}{C+T} = 1$) follows from base pairing across two strands of double-stranded DNA; single-stranded RNA does not obey it, while RNA still has complementary base pairing (e.g. tRNA), $5'$ phosphoryl and $3'$ hydroxyl ends, and heterocyclic nitrogenous bases, so option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Salient Features of Structure of DNA · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 32)

![printed question 120](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q120_a31e287f_28102026.png?v=a31e287ffd17)

## Q121

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

Doctors use stethoscope to hear the sound; produced during each cardiac cycle. The second sound is heard when:

- (1) AV node receives signal from SA node
- (2) AV valves open up
- (3) Ventricular walls vibrate due to gushing of blood from atria
- **(4)** Semilunar valves close down after the blood flows into vessels from ventricles

1. Second heart sound is 'DUP' which is produced during early ventricular diastole due to the sharp closure of semilunar valves.

## Q122

Biology · botany · Ecosystem

During ecological succession:

- (1) The changes lead to a community that is in near equilibrium with the environment and is called pioneer community
- **(2)** The gradual and predictable change in species composition occurs in a given area
- (3) The establishment of a new biotic community is very fast in its primary phase
- (4) The number and types of animals remain constant

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Ecological succession involves gradual and fairly predictable change in the species composition of a given area.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Ecological succession is defined as the gradual and fairly predictable change in species composition of a given area, so option $2$ is correct; the near-equilibrium community is the climax community (option $1$ false), primary succession is very slow (option $3$ false), and the number and types of animals change (option $4$ false).

## Q123

Biology · both · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

In the following human pedigree, the filled symbols represent the affected individuals. Identify the type of given pedigree.

![printed figure: question](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q123_e652acd2_28102026.png?v=e652acd2a8c4)

- (1) X-linked dominant
- (2) Autosomal dominant
- (3) X-linked recessive
- **(4)** Autosomal recessive

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The given pedigree represents inheritance of Autosomal recessive trait.

![printed figure: solution_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q123_sol_1_baac6b50_28102026.png?v=baac6b50e20b)


**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In generation II, an unaffected man and an unaffected woman have an affected son in III, and in generation III two unaffected parents have two affected sons in IV, so the trait is recessive (dominant traits cannot skip from unaffected parents).
2. The affected woman in II ($X^aX^a$ if X-linked recessive) would pass $X^a$ to every son, so all her sons would be affected; her son in III is unaffected, so the trait is not X-linked recessive.
3. All matings fit autosomal recessive: affected $aa$ father I x carrier $Aa$ mother gives $aa$ and $Aa$ children; carrier $Aa \times Aa$ couples give $aa$ affected sons, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Pedigree Analysis · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 32)

![printed question 123](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q123_bd9ebbc8_28102026.png?v=bd9ebbc80bed)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 123](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q123_5de57708_28102026.png?v=5de5770869c2)

## Q127

Biology · both · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Satellite DNA is important because it :

- (1) Codes for enzymes needed for DNA replication
- (2) Codes for proteins needed in cell cycle
- **(3)** Shows high degree of polymorphism in population and also the same degree of polymorphism in an individual, which is heritable from parents to children
- (4) Does not code for proteins and is same in all members of the population

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Satellite DNA are the repetitive DNA which do not code for any protein.
2. They show high degree of polymorphism and form basis of DNA fingerprinting.
3. Since DNA from every tissue from an individual show the same degree of polymorphism, they become very useful identification tool in forensic applications.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Satellite DNA is highly repetitive DNA that does not code for any protein, so options $1$ and $2$ are wrong.
2. Satellite DNA shows a high degree of polymorphism between individuals of a population, so option $4$ ('same in all members') is wrong.
3. These polymorphisms are inherited from parents to children, which makes satellite DNA the basis of DNA fingerprinting, so the answer is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** DNA Fingerprinting · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 34)

![printed question 127](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q127_c72949ce_28102026.png?v=c72949ceb65c)

## Q129

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

A column of water within xylem vessels of tall trees does not break under its weight because of :

- (1) Positive root pressure
- (2) Dissolved sugars in water
- **(3)** Tensile strength of water
- (4) Lignification of xylem vessels

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The column of water within Xylem vessel of tall trees does not break under its weight due to high tensile strength of water.
2. Tensile strength is the ability to resist pulling forces.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. By the cohesion-tension (transpiration pull) model, water molecules cohere strongly, giving water a high tensile strength, so the column in the xylem resists breaking under its own weight; this is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Transpiration and Stomatal Opening and Closing · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q130

Biology · both · Biotechnology: Principles and Processes

The introduction of t-DNA into plants involves:

- (1) Allowing the plant roots to stand in water
- **(2)** Infection of the plant by Agrobacterium tumefaciens
- (3) Altering the pH of the soil, then heat shocking the plants
- (4) Exposing the plants to cold for a brief period

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. When Agrobacterium tumifaciens infects the host plant, it will transfer a part of DNA called t-DNA without any human interference so called natural genetic engineer.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. T-DNA is part of the Ti plasmid of $Agrobacterium\ tumefaciens$, and the bacterium transfers it into the plant genome when it infects a dicot plant, so the answer is option $2$.

## Q136

Physics · Modern Physics

In the spectrum of hydrogen, the ratio of the longest wavelength in the Lyman series to the longest wavelength in the Balmer series is:

- **(1)** $\frac{5}{27}$
- (2) $\frac{4}{9}$
- (3) $\frac{9}{4}$
- (4) $\frac{27}{5}$

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\frac{1}{\lambda_1} = \mathrm{R_e}\left(\frac{1}{1^2} - \frac{1}{2^2}\right)$
2. $\frac{1}{\lambda_2} = \mathrm{R_e}\left(\frac{1}{2^2} - \frac{1}{3^2}\right)$
3. $\frac{\lambda_1}{\lambda_2} = \frac{5}{27}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The longest wavelength in a series comes from the smallest energy gap, so Lyman uses $n=2 \to 1$ and Balmer uses $n=3 \to 2$.
2. $\frac{1}{\lambda_L} = R\left(1 - \frac{1}{4}\right) = \frac{3R}{4}$, so $\lambda_L = \frac{4}{3R}$.
3. $\frac{1}{\lambda_B} = R\left(\frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{9}\right) = \frac{5R}{36}$, so $\lambda_B = \frac{36}{5R}$.
4. $\frac{\lambda_L}{\lambda_B} = \frac{4}{3R} \times \frac{5R}{36} = \frac{20}{108} = \frac{5}{27}$, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Spectral Lines of Hydrogen Atom · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 36)

![printed question 136](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q136_9455c444_28102026.png?v=9455c4440dd5)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 136](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q136_22361e9a_28102026.png?v=22361e9a1993)

## Q137

Physics · Electromagnetic Waves

The energy of the em waves is of the order of $15\,\mathrm{keV}$. To which part of the spectrum does it belong?

- (1) $\gamma$-rays
- **(2)** X-rays
- (3) Infra-red rays
- (4) Ultraviolet rays

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Wavelength of the ray
2. $\lambda = \frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\mathrm{E}}$
3. $= 0.826\,\mathring{\mathrm{A}}$
4. Since $\lambda < 100\,\mathring{\mathrm{A}}$
5. So it is X-ray

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Use $\lambda = \frac{hc}{E}$ with $hc \approx 1240\,\mathrm{eV\,nm}$.
2. $\lambda = \frac{1240\,\mathrm{eV\,nm}}{15\times10^{3}\,\mathrm{eV}} \approx 0.083\,\mathrm{nm} \approx 0.83\,\text{\AA}$.
3. X-rays span roughly $10\,\mathrm{nm}$ to $10^{-4}\,\mathrm{nm}$, while $\gamma$-rays are below about $10^{-3}\,\mathrm{nm}$ and UV/IR are far longer, so $0.083\,\mathrm{nm}$ is X-rays, option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Electromagnetic Spectrum · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 36)

![printed question 137](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q137_cf9a3640_28102026.png?v=cf9a3640fd09)

## Q138

Physics · Electromagnetic Induction

An electron moves on a straight line path XY as shown. The abcd is a coil adjacent to the path of electron. What will be the direction of current, if any, induced in the coil?

![printed figure: A circular coil abcd above the straight path XY of an electron](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q138_d7bd64ca_28102026.png?v=d7bd64ca425a)

*A circular coil abcd above the straight path XY of an electron*

- (1) No current induced
- (2) abcd
- (3) adcb
- **(4)** The current will reverse its direction as the electron goes past the coil

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

![printed figure: An electron e⁻ passing along a straight line below a circular coil](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q138_sol_1_b3acab72_28102026.png?v=b3acab729688)

*An electron e⁻ passing along a straight line below a circular coil*

1. When $e^{-}$ comes closer the induced current will be anticlockwise
2. When $e^{-}$ comes farther induced current will be clockwise.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The electron moves from $X$ to $Y$, so the equivalent conventional current $I$ flows from $Y$ to $X$ (to the left).
2. For a leftward current, the field at points above the line is $\vec B \propto I\,d\vec l \times \hat r = (-\hat x)\times(+\hat y) = -\hat z$, i.e. into the page through the coil.
3. While the electron approaches the coil, $\Phi_B$ (into the page) increases, so by Lenz's law the induced current makes a field out of the page: anticlockwise, $a \to b \to c \to d$.
4. After the electron passes the coil, $\Phi_B$ decreases, so the induced current makes a field into the page: clockwise, $a \to d \to c \to b$.
5. The induced current reverses direction as the electron goes past the coil, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Direction of Induced Current · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 36)

![printed question 138](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q138_9607484c_28102026.png?v=9607484cde11)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 138](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q138_0abc8cfe_28102026.png?v=0abc8cfe91c1)

## Q139

Physics · Mechanical Properties of Fluids

The cylindrical tube of a spray pump has radius $R$, one end of which has $n$ fine holes, each of radius $r$. If the speed of the liquid in the tube is $V$, the speed of the ejection of the liquid through the holes is :

- (1) $\frac{V^{2}R}{nr}$
- (2) $\frac{VR^{2}}{n^{2}r^{2}}$
- **(3)** $\frac{VR^{2}}{nr^{2}}$
- (4) $\frac{VR^{2}}{n^{3}r^{2}}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Volume inflow rate = volume anflow rate
2. $\pi R^{2}V = n\pi r^{2} \quad \Rightarrow \quad v = \frac{\pi R^{2}V}{n\pi r^{2}} = \frac{VR^{2}}{\mathrm{nr}^{2}}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. By the equation of continuity, the volume flow rate in the tube equals the total volume flow rate through the $n$ holes: $A_1 V = n A_2 v$.
2. Substitute the areas: $\pi R^{2} V = n \pi r^{2} v$.
3. Solve for $v$: $v = \frac{V R^{2}}{n r^{2}}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Equation of Continuity and Bernoulli's Equation · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 37)

![printed question 139](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q139_de0477ca_28102026.png?v=de0477caa7d9)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 139](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q139_e6cc26c4_28102026.png?v=e6cc26c46410)

## Q140

Physics · Mechanical Properties of Solids

The Young’s modulus of steel is twice that of brass. Two wires of same length and of same area of cross section, one of steel and another of brass are suspended from the same roof. If we want the lower ends of the wires to be at the same level, then the weights added to the steel and brass wires must be in the ration of:

- (1) $1 : 1$
- (2) $1 : 2$
- **(3)** $2 : 1$
- (4) $4 : 2$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

![printed figure: Brass and steel wires of length l and area A hanging from the same roof](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q140_sol_1_def0c676_28102026.png?v=def0c676acc8)

*Brass and steel wires of length l and area A hanging from the same roof*

1. $\mathrm{Y} = \frac{\mathrm{W}}{\mathrm{A}} \cdot \frac{\ell}{\Delta\ell}$
2. So $\Delta\ell = \frac{\mathrm{W}\ell}{\mathrm{AY}}$
3. $\Delta \mathrm{e}_1 = \Delta \mathrm{e}_2$
4. $\frac{\mathrm{w}_1\ell}{\mathrm{AY}_1} = \frac{\mathrm{w}_2\ell}{\mathrm{AY}_2}$
5. $\frac{\mathrm{w}_1}{\mathrm{w}_2} = \frac{\mathrm{Y}_1}{\mathrm{Y}_2} = 2$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Extension of a wire under load $W$: $\Delta L = \frac{W L}{A Y}$.
2. Lower ends at the same level means $\Delta L_s = \Delta L_b$, with the same $L$ and $A$, so $\frac{W_s}{Y_s} = \frac{W_b}{Y_b}$.
3. $\frac{W_s}{W_b} = \frac{Y_s}{Y_b} = \frac{2Y_b}{Y_b} = 2$, i.e. $2 : 1$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Hooke's Law · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 37)

![printed question 140](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q140_89ac7759_28102026.png?v=89ac775987a0)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 140](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q140_ff02dd8a_28102026.png?v=ff02dd8a2c1b)

## Q141

Physics · Current Electricity

A potentiometer wire of length $L$ and a resistance $r$ are connected in series with a battery of e.m.f. $E_0$ and a resistance $r_1$. An unknown e.m.f. $E$ is balanced at a length $l$ of the potentiometer wire. The e.m.f. $E$ will be given by:

- (1) $\dfrac{LE_0r}{(r+r_1)l}$
- (2) $\dfrac{LE_0r}{lr_1}$
- **(3)** $\dfrac{E_0r}{(r+r_1)}\cdot\dfrac{l}{L}$
- (4) $\dfrac{E_0l}{L}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $K = $ potential gradient $= \left(\frac{E_0 r}{r + r_1}\right)\frac{1}{L}$
2. So $E = K\ell = \frac{E_0 r \ell}{(r + r_1)L}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Current in the series circuit: $I = \frac{E_0}{r + r_1}$.
2. Potential drop across the whole wire: $V = I r = \frac{E_0 r}{r + r_1}$.
3. Potential gradient: $k = \frac{V}{L} = \frac{E_0 r}{(r + r_1)L}$.
4. At balance: $E = k l = \frac{E_0 r}{(r + r_1)} \cdot \frac{l}{L}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Potentiometer · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 38)

![printed question 141](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q141_4bc7cf03_28102026.png?v=4bc7cf0325b0)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 141](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q141_dd0311fd_28102026.png?v=dd0311fd1add)

## Q142

Physics · Oscillations

A particle is executing a simple harmonic motion. Its maximum acceleration is $\alpha$ and maximum velocity is $\beta$. Then, its time period of vibration will be:

- **(1)** $\frac{2\pi\beta}{\alpha}$
- (2) $\frac{\beta^2}{\alpha^2}$
- (3) $\frac{\alpha}{\beta}$
- (4) $\frac{\beta^2}{\alpha}$

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\omega^{2}A = \alpha$
2. $\omega A = \beta$
3. $\Rightarrow \omega = \frac{\alpha}{\beta}$
4. $\Rightarrow T = \frac{2\pi}{\omega} = \frac{2\pi\beta}{\alpha}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In SHM, maximum acceleration $\alpha = A\omega^2$ and maximum velocity $\beta = A\omega$.
2. Dividing, $\frac{\alpha}{\beta} = \frac{A\omega^2}{A\omega} = \omega$.
3. $T = \frac{2\pi}{\omega} = \frac{2\pi\beta}{\alpha}$, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Calculate Time Period of SHM · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 38)

![printed question 142](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q142_29d4947d_28102026.png?v=29d4947d8125)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 142](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q142_3cadb7d1_28102026.png?v=3cadb7d1df8c)

## Q143

Physics · Kinematics

If vectors $\vec{A} = \cos \omega t\, i\hat{i} + \sin\omega t\,\hat{j}$ and $\vec{B} = \cos \frac{\omega t}{2}\hat{i} + \sin \frac{\omega t}{2}\hat{j}$ are functions of times, then the value of t at which they are orthogonal to each other is:

- (1) $t = 0$
- (2) $t = \frac{\pi}{4\omega}$
- (3) $t = \frac{\pi}{2\omega}$
- **(4)** $t = \frac{\pi}{\omega}$

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\bar{A} = \cos wt\,\hat{i} + \sin wt\,\hat{j}$
2. $\bar{B} = \cos\frac{wt}{2}\,\hat{i} + \sin\frac{wt}{2}\,\hat{j}$
3. for $\vec{A}\,.\,\vec{B} = 0$
4. $\bar{A}\,.\,\bar{B} = 0 = \cos wt.\cos\frac{wt}{2} + \sin wt.\sin\frac{wt}{2}$
5. $= \cos\left(wt - \frac{wt}{2}\right) = \cos\left(\frac{wt}{2}\right)$
6. So $\frac{wt}{2} = \frac{\pi}{2} \Rightarrow t = \frac{\pi}{w}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Orthogonality requires $\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = 0$.
2. $\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = \cos\omega t\cos\frac{\omega t}{2} + \sin\omega t\sin\frac{\omega t}{2}$.
3. Using $\cos(a-b)$: $\vec{A}\cdot\vec{B} = \cos\left(\omega t - \frac{\omega t}{2}\right) = \cos\frac{\omega t}{2}$.
4. $\cos\frac{\omega t}{2} = 0 \Rightarrow \frac{\omega t}{2} = \frac{\pi}{2}$ (first non-zero time).
5. $t = \frac{\pi}{\omega}$, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Dot Product · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 38)

![printed question 143](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q143_32576579_28102026.png?v=32576579e2db)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 143](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q143_0e960800_28102026.png?v=0e9608007c9d)

## Q144

Physics · Waves

A source of sound S emitting waves of frequency $100\,\mathrm{Hz}$ and an observer O are located at some distance from each other. The source is moving with a speed of $19.4\,\mathrm{ms^{-1}}$ at an angle of $60^{\circ}$ with the source observer line as shown in the figure. The observer is at rest. The apparent frequency observed by the observer (velocity of sound in air $330\,\mathrm{ms^{-1}}$), is:

![printed figure: Source S moving with velocity V_S at 60 degrees to the line from S to observer O](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q144_1e4da28b_28102026.png?v=1e4da28b0c2f)

*Source S moving with velocity V_S at 60 degrees to the line from S to observer O*

- (1) $97\,\mathrm{Hz}$
- (2) $100\,\mathrm{Hz}$
- **(3)** $103\,\mathrm{Hz}$
- (4) $106\,\mathrm{Hz}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

![printed figure: Source S velocity 19.4 at 60 degrees to the line S-O, with its component 19.4 cos60° = 9.7 along S-O](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q144_sol_1_168f2c8d_28102026.png?v=168f2c8d4791)

*Source S velocity 19.4 at 60 degrees to the line S-O, with its component 19.4 cos60° = 9.7 along S-O*

1. $19.4\cos 60^{\circ} = 9.7$
2. $t^{1} = f_{0}\left(\frac{v - v}{v - v_{s}}\right)$
3. $f^{1} = 100\left(\frac{v - 0}{v - (+9.7)}\right)$
4. $f^{1} = 100\frac{v}{v\left(1 - \frac{9.7}{v}\right)}$
5. $f^{1} = 100\left(1 + \frac{3.7}{330}\right) = 103\mathrm{Hz}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. From the figure the source moves at $60^{\circ}$ to the line SO, with a component directed towards O.
2. Component of source velocity along the source-observer line: $v_s\cos 60^{\circ} = 19.4 \times \frac{1}{2} = 9.7\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-1}}$.
3. Source approaching a stationary observer: $f' = f\,\frac{v}{v - v_s\cos 60^{\circ}}$.
4. $f' = 100 \times \frac{330}{330 - 9.7} = 100 \times \frac{330}{320.3} \approx 103\,\mathrm{Hz}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Doppler Effect · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

## Q145

Physics · Rotational Motion

An automobile moves on a road with a speed of $54\,\mathrm{km\,h^{-1}}$. The radius of its wheels is $0.45\,\mathrm{m}$ and the moment of inertia of the wheel about its axis of rotation is $3\,\mathrm{kg\,m^{2}}$. If the vehicle is brought to rest in $15\,\mathrm{s}$, the magnitude of average torque transmitted by its brakes to the wheel is:

- (1) $2.86\,\mathrm{kg\,m^{2}\,s^{-2}}$
- **(2)** $6.66\,\mathrm{kg\,m^{2}\,s^{-2}}$
- (3) $8.58\,\mathrm{kg\,m^{2}\,s^{-2}}$
- (4) $10.86\,\mathrm{kg\,m^{2}\,s^{-2}}$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\omega_{i} = \frac{15}{0.45} = \frac{100}{3}$ $\omega_{f} = 0$
2. $\omega_{f} = \omega_{i} + \alpha t$
3. $0 = \frac{100}{3} + (-\alpha)(15)$
4. $\alpha = \frac{100}{45}$
5. $\tau = (I)(\alpha) = 3 \times \frac{100}{45} = 6.66\,\mathrm{N.M}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $v = 54\,\mathrm{km\,h^{-1}} = 54 \times \frac{5}{18} = 15\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-1}}$.
2. $\omega_0 = \frac{v}{r} = \frac{15}{0.45} = 33.33\,\mathrm{rad\,s^{-1}}$.
3. $\alpha = \frac{\omega_0 - 0}{t} = \frac{33.33}{15} = 2.22\,\mathrm{rad\,s^{-2}}$.
4. $\tau = I\alpha = 3 \times 2.22 = 6.66\,\mathrm{kg\,m^{2}\,s^{-2}}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Problems on Torque of a Force About the Axis of Rotation · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 40)

![printed question 145](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q145_41e2a092_28102026.png?v=41e2a092a617)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 145](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q145_a5ef81cf_28102026.png?v=a5ef81cf1be7)

## Q146

Physics · Magnetic Effect of Electric Current

A rectangular coil of length $0.12\,\mathrm{m}$ and width $0.1\,\mathrm{m}$ having 50 turns of wire is suspended vertically in a uniform magnetic field of strength $0.2\,\mathrm{Weber/m^2}$. The coil carries a current of $2\,\mathrm{A}$. if the plane of the coil is inclined at an angle of $30^{\circ}$ with the direction of the field, the torque required to keep coil in stable equilibrium will be:

- (1) $0.12\,\mathrm{Nm}$
- (2) $0.15\,\mathrm{Nm}$
- **(3)** $0.20\,\mathrm{Nm}$
- (4) $0.24\,\mathrm{Nm}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

![printed figure: Rectangular coil (seen as an ellipse) whose plane makes 30 degrees with field B; magnetic moment M at 60 degrees to B](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q146_sol_1_1610ba69_28102026.png?v=1610ba6994cd)

*Rectangular coil (seen as an ellipse) whose plane makes 30 degrees with field B; magnetic moment M at 60 degrees to B*

1. $\vec{\tau} = \vec{M} \times \bar{B} = MB\sin 60^{\circ}$
2. $= Ni\,AB\sin 60^{\circ}$
3. $50 \times 2 \times 0.12 \times 0.1 \times 0.2 \times \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$
4. $= 12\sqrt{3} \times 10^{-2}\,\mathrm{Nm} = 0.20748\,\mathrm{Nm}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Area of the coil: $A = 0.12 \times 0.1 = 0.012\,\mathrm{m^2}$.
2. Magnetic moment: $m = NIA = 50 \times 2 \times 0.012 = 1.2\,\mathrm{A\,m^2}$.
3. The plane makes $30^{\circ}$ with $\vec{B}$, so the area vector (normal) makes $\theta = 90^{\circ} - 30^{\circ} = 60^{\circ}$ with $\vec{B}$.
4. $\tau = mB\sin\theta = 1.2 \times 0.2 \times \sin 60^{\circ}$.
5. $\tau = 0.24 \times 0.866 = 0.208\,\mathrm{N\,m} \approx 0.20\,\mathrm{N\,m}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Torque on Current Loop · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 40)

![printed question 146](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q146_42b26711_28102026.png?v=42b26711c569)

## Q147

Physics · Capacitance

A parallel plate air capacitor has capacity ‘$C$’, distance of separation between plates is ‘$d$’ and potential difference ‘$V$’ is applied between the plates. Force of attraction between the plates of the parallel plate air capacitor is:

- (1) $\frac{C^2V^2}{2\,d^2}$
- (2) $\frac{C^2V^2}{2\,d}$
- **(3)** $\frac{CV^2}{2d}$
- (4) $\frac{CV^2}{d}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Attraction between the plates
2. $\mathrm{F} = \frac{q^{2}}{2A\varepsilon_{0}}$ where $q - CV$ and $C = \frac{\varepsilon_{0}A}{d}$
3. $\mathrm{F} = \frac{C^{2}V^{2}}{2Cd} = \frac{CV^{2}}{2d}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Each plate is in the field of the other plate only, $E' = \frac{\sigma}{2\varepsilon_0}$, so $F = Q E' = \frac{Q^2}{2\varepsilon_0 A}$.
2. Substitute $Q = CV$: $F = \frac{C^2V^2}{2\varepsilon_0 A}$.
3. Use $C = \frac{\varepsilon_0 A}{d}$, so $\varepsilon_0 A = Cd$: $F = \frac{C^2V^2}{2Cd} = \frac{CV^2}{2d}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** The Parallel Plate Capacitor · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 41)

![printed question 147](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q147_334dd7a5_28102026.png?v=334dd7a5fe99)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 147](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q147_a16bed2a_28102026.png?v=a16bed2a59c5)

## Q148

Physics · Thermodynamics

Two vessels separately contain two ideal gases A and B at the same temperature, the pressure of a being twice that of B. Under such conditions, the density of A is found to be 1.5 times the density if B. The ratio of molecular weight of A and B is:

- (1) $\frac{1}{2}$
- (2) $\frac{2}{3}$
- **(3)** $\frac{3}{4}$
- (4) $2$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{P_A} = \frac{\rho_{\mathrm{A}}\mathrm{M_A}}{\mathrm{RT}}, \mathrm{P_B} = \frac{\rho_{\mathrm{B}}\mathrm{M_B}}{\mathrm{RT}} = \frac{3}{2} \Rightarrow \frac{\mathrm{P_A}}{\mathrm{P_B}} = \frac{\rho_{\mathrm{A}}}{\rho_{\mathrm{B}}}\frac{\mathrm{M_A}}{\mathrm{M_B}} = 2\frac{\mathrm{M_A}}{\mathrm{M_B}} = \frac{3}{2}$
2. So, $\frac{\mathrm{M_A}}{\mathrm{M_B}} = \frac{3}{4}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Ideal gas law in density form: $PM = \rho R T$, so $M = \frac{\rho R T}{P}$.
2. At the same $T$: $\frac{M_A}{M_B} = \frac{\rho_A}{\rho_B} \cdot \frac{P_B}{P_A}$.
3. Substitute $\rho_A = 1.5\,\rho_B$ and $P_A = 2P_B$: $\frac{M_A}{M_B} = 1.5 \times \frac{1}{2} = \frac{3}{4}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Ideal Gas Equation · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 41)

![printed question 148](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q148_82c99610_28102026.png?v=82c99610748b)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 148](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q148_09a9c41a_28102026.png?v=09a9c41a6d62)

## Q149

Physics · Gravitation

A satellite S is moving in an elliptical orbit around the earth. The mass of the satellite is very small compared to the mass of the earth. Then,

- **(1)** The acceleration of S is always directed towards the centre of the earth
- (2) The angular momentum of S about the centre of the earth changes in direction, but its magnitude remains constant
- (3) The total mechanical energy of S varies periodically with time
- (4) The linear momentum of S remains constant is magnitude

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The gravitation force on the satellite will be aiming toward the centre of earth so acceleration of the satellite will also be aiming toward the centre of earth.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The only force on $S$ is gravity, $\vec F = -\frac{GMm}{r^2}\hat r$, which points to the earth's centre, so $\vec a = \vec F/m$ always points to the centre (option $1$ true).
2. Torque about the centre is $\vec\tau = \vec r \times \vec F = 0$, so $\vec L$ is constant in both magnitude and direction (option $2$ false).
3. Gravity is conservative, so $E = \frac{1}{2}mv^2 - \frac{GMm}{r}$ stays constant (option $3$ false).
4. Since $L = mvr_\perp$ is constant and the distance changes along the ellipse, $v$ changes, so $|\vec p| = mv$ is not constant (option $4$ false).

**Subtopic:** Motion of a Satellite · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 41)

![printed question 149](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q149_f627cab3_28102026.png?v=f627cab33268)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 149](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q149_776ccda3_28102026.png?v=776ccda3b89e)

## Q150

Physics · Semiconductor Electronics

In the given figure, a diode D is connected to an external resistance $R = 100\,\Omega$ and an e.m.f. of $3.5\,\mathrm{V}$. If the barrier potential developed across the diode is $0.5\,\mathrm{V}$, the current in the circuit will be:

![printed figure: A diode D in series with a 100 ohm resistor R and a 3.5 V cell](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q150_492d5187_28102026.png?v=492d51876825)

*A diode D in series with a 100 ohm resistor R and a 3.5 V cell*

- (1) $35\,\mathrm{mA}$
- **(2)** $30\,\mathrm{mA}$
- (3) $40\,\mathrm{mA}$
- (4) $20\,\mathrm{mA}$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\text{Current} = \frac{(3.5 - 0.5)}{100}\,\mathrm{A}$
2. $= \frac{3}{100}\,\mathrm{A} = 30\,\mathrm{mA}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The diode is in series with $R$ and is taken as forward biased (no option corresponds to zero current), so the barrier potential $0.5\,\mathrm{V}$ is dropped across it.
2. Voltage across the resistor: $V_R = 3.5\,\mathrm{V} - 0.5\,\mathrm{V} = 3.0\,\mathrm{V}$.
3. $I = \frac{V_R}{R} = \frac{3.0\,\mathrm{V}}{100\,\Omega} = 0.03\,\mathrm{A} = 30\,\mathrm{mA}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** PN Junction Diode Under Forward Bias · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 42)

![printed question 150](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q150_f32deff1_28102026.png?v=f32deff151f4)

## Q151

Physics · Gravitation

A remote-sensing satellite of earth revolves in a circular orbit at a height of $0.25 \times 10^{6}\,\mathrm{m}$ above the surface of earth. If earth’s radius is $6.38 \times 10^{6}\,\mathrm{m}$ and $g = 9.8\,\mathrm{ms^{-2}}$, then the orbital speed of the satellite is:

- (1) $6.67\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$
- **(2)** $7.76\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$
- (3) $8.56\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$
- (4) $9.13\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $V_0 = \sqrt{\frac{GM}{r}} = \sqrt{\frac{GM}{R^2}.\frac{R^2}{r}}$
2. $= \sqrt{\frac{9.8\times6.38\times6.38}{6.63\times10^{6}}} = \sqrt{60\times10^{6}}\,\mathrm{m/sec}$
3. $= 7.76\,\mathrm{km/sec}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Orbit radius: $r = R + h = 6.38 \times 10^{6} + 0.25 \times 10^{6} = 6.63 \times 10^{6}\,\mathrm{m}$.
2. Gravity provides the centripetal force: $\frac{mv^2}{r} = \frac{GMm}{r^2}$, so $v = \sqrt{\frac{GM}{r}}$.
3. Use $GM = gR^2$: $v = R\sqrt{\frac{g}{r}}$.
4. $\frac{g}{r} = \frac{9.8}{6.63 \times 10^{6}} = 1.478 \times 10^{-6}\,\mathrm{s^{-2}}$, so $\sqrt{\frac{g}{r}} = 1.216 \times 10^{-3}\,\mathrm{s^{-1}}$.
5. $v = 6.38 \times 10^{6} \times 1.216 \times 10^{-3} \approx 7.76 \times 10^{3}\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-1}} = 7.76\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Orbital Speed · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 42)

![printed question 151](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q151_8eea4043_28102026.png?v=8eea40437514)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 151](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q151_1f95b422_28102026.png?v=1f95b4223ad4)

## Q152

Physics · Kinematics

The position vector of a particle $\vec{\mathrm{R}}$ as a function of time is given by:
$\bar{\mathrm{R}} = 4\sin(2\pi t)\,\hat{i} + 4\cos(2\pi t)\,\hat{j}$
Where R is in meters, t is in seconds and $\hat{i}$ and $\hat{j}$ denote unit vectors along x- and y-directions, respectively. Which one of the following statements is wrong for the motion of particle?

- (1) Patch of the particle is a circle of radius $4$ meter
- (2) Acceleration vector is along $-\bar{\mathrm{R}}$
- (3) Magnitude of acceleration vector is $\frac{v^2}{R}$, where v is the velocity of particle
- **(4)** Magnitude of the velocity of particle is $8$ meter/second

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $x = 45\,\mathrm{m}\;2\pi t,$
2. $y = 4\cos(2\pi t)$
3. Squiring and adding
4. $\Rightarrow$ Circular motion
5. $V = \omega = (2\pi)(4) = 8\pi$
6. So , Ans is (4)

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $|\vec{R}| = \sqrt{16\sin^2(2\pi t) + 16\cos^2(2\pi t)} = 4\,\text{m}$, so the path is a circle of radius $4\,\text{m}$; statement 1 is true.
2. $\vec{v} = \frac{d\vec{R}}{dt} = 8\pi\cos(2\pi t)\,\hat{i} - 8\pi\sin(2\pi t)\,\hat{j}$, so $|\vec{v}| = 8\pi\,\text{m/s}$, not $8\,\text{m/s}$; statement 4 is wrong.
3. $\vec{a} = \frac{d\vec{v}}{dt} = -16\pi^2\sin(2\pi t)\,\hat{i} - 16\pi^2\cos(2\pi t)\,\hat{j} = -4\pi^2\vec{R}$, so $\vec{a}$ is along $-\vec{R}$; statement 2 is true.
4. $|\vec{a}| = 16\pi^2$ and $\frac{v^2}{R} = \frac{64\pi^2}{4} = 16\pi^2$, so statement 3 is true.
5. The only wrong statement is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Uniform Circular Motion · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 42)

![printed question 152](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q152_a24bbbdb_28102026.png?v=a24bbbdbd989)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 152](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q152_22c3d259_28102026.png?v=22c3d2595b00)

## Q153

Physics · Waves

A string is stretched between fixed points separated by $75.0\,\mathrm{cm}$. It is observed to have resonant frequencies of $420\,\mathrm{Hz}$ and $315\,\mathrm{Hz}$. There are no other resonant frequency for this string is:

- **(1)** $105\,\mathrm{Hz}$
- (2) $155\,\mathrm{Hz}$
- (3) $205\,\mathrm{Hz}$
- (4) $105\,\mathrm{Hz}$

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Two consecutive resonant frequencies for a string fixed at both ends will be
2. $\frac{nv}{2\ell}$ and $\frac{(n+1)v}{2\ell}$
3. $\Rightarrow \frac{(n+1)v}{2\ell} - \frac{nv}{2\ell} = 420 - 315$
4. $\frac{v}{2\ell} = 105\,\mathrm{Hz}$
5. Which is the minimum resonant frequency.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. For a string fixed at both ends, $f_n = n f_1$, so consecutive resonant frequencies differ by $f_1$.
2. $f_1 = 420\,\mathrm{Hz} - 315\,\mathrm{Hz} = 105\,\mathrm{Hz}$.
3. Check: $315 = 3 \times 105$ and $420 = 4 \times 105$, so they are the 3rd and 4th harmonics.
4. The lowest resonant frequency is $f_1 = 105\,\mathrm{Hz}$, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Standing Wave on a String Fixed at Both Ends · **Difficulty:** unsure (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 43)

![printed question 153](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q153_05e49ede_28102026.png?v=05e49edea505)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

## Q154

Physics · Rotational Motion

Point masses $\mathrm{m_1}$ and $\mathrm{m_2}$ are placed at the opposite ends of rigid rod of length L, and negligible mass. The rod is to be set rotating about an axis perpendicular to it. The position of point P on this rod through which the axis should pass so that the work required to set the rod rotating with angular velocity $\omega_0$ is minimum, is given by:

![printed figure: Rod of length L with masses m1 and m2 at its ends, rotating about a perpendicular axis through P](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q154_5cd0a563_28102026.png?v=5cd0a563672b)

*Rod of length L with masses m1 and m2 at its ends, rotating about a perpendicular axis through P*

- **(1)** $\mathrm{x} = \frac{\mathrm{m_2 L}}{\mathrm{m_1 + m_2}}$
- (2) $\mathrm{x} = \frac{\mathrm{m_1 L}}{\mathrm{m_! + m_2}}$
- (3) $\mathrm{x} = \frac{\mathrm{m_1}}{\mathrm{m_2}}\mathrm{L}$
- (4) $\mathrm{x} = \frac{\mathrm{m_2}}{\mathrm{m_1}}\mathrm{L}$

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{K.\,E.} = \frac{1}{2}\mathrm{I}\,\omega^{2}$
2. $\mathrm{I}$ is min. about the centre of mass
3. So, $(\mathrm{m_1})(\mathrm{x}) = (\mathrm{m_2})(\mathrm{L} - \mathrm{x})$
4. $\mathrm{x} = \frac{\mathrm{m_2 L}}{\mathrm{m_1 + m_2}}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Work needed equals the rotational kinetic energy: $W = \frac{1}{2} I \omega_0^2$, so $W$ is minimum when $I$ is minimum.
2. From the figure, $x$ is the distance of P from $m_1$: $I = m_1 x^2 + m_2 (L-x)^2$.
3. $\frac{dI}{dx} = 2 m_1 x - 2 m_2 (L-x) = 0$.
4. $x(m_1 + m_2) = m_2 L \Rightarrow x = \frac{m_2 L}{m_1 + m_2}$, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Calculation of Moment of Inertia, Kinetic Energy of a Rigid Body Rotating About a Given Axis · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 43)

![printed question 154](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q154_f7f4ddd4_28102026.png?v=f7f4ddd4e4d8)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 154](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q154_f01ec922_28102026.png?v=f01ec922f890)

## Q155

Physics · Wave Optics

At the first minimum adjacent to the central maximum of a single-slit diffraction pattern, the phase difference between the Huygen’s wavelet from the edge of the slit and the wavelet from the midpoint of the slit is:

- (1) $\frac{\pi}{8}$ radian
- (2) $\frac{\pi}{4}$ radian
- (3) $\frac{\pi}{2}$ radian
- **(4)** $\pi$ radian

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. For first minima
2. $\mathrm{AP} - \mathrm{BP} = \lambda$

![printed figure: Single slit with edge A, midpoint M and edge B, rays from A and B meeting at P on the screen](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q155_sol_1_1e398762_28102026.png?v=1e398762183e)

*Single slit with edge A, midpoint M and edge B, rays from A and B meeting at P on the screen*

3. $\mathrm{AP} - \mathrm{MP} = \frac{\lambda}{2}$
4. So phase difference $= \frac{2\pi}{\lambda} \times \frac{\lambda}{2} = \pi$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. First minimum of a single slit: $a\sin\theta = \lambda$.
2. Edge and midpoint are $a/2$ apart, so their path difference is $\frac{a}{2}\sin\theta = \frac{\lambda}{2}$.
3. Phase difference $\phi = \frac{2\pi}{\lambda}\cdot\frac{\lambda}{2} = \pi$ radian, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Diffraction of Light · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 44)

## Q156

Physics · Rotational Motion

A force $\vec{\mathrm{F}} = \alpha\hat{\imath} + 3\hat{\jmath} + 6\hat{k}$ is acting at a point $\vec{\mathrm{r}} = 2\hat{\imath} - 6\hat{\jmath} - 12\hat{k}$. The value of $\alpha$ for which angular momentum about origin is conserved is:

- (1) $-$
- **(2)** $-1$
- (3) $2$
- (4) Zero

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. If $\vec{\mathrm{L}} = \text{constant}$ then $\vec{\tau} = 0$
2. So $\vec{\mathrm{r}} \times \vec{\mathrm{F}} = 0 \Rightarrow \vec{\mathrm{F}}$ should be parallel to $\vec{\mathrm{r}}$ so coefficient should be in same ratio. So $\frac{\alpha}{2} = \frac{3}{-6} = \frac{6}{-12}$
3. So $\alpha = -1$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Angular momentum about the origin is conserved when the torque $\vec{\tau} = \vec{r} \times \vec{F} = 0$.
2. $\vec{r} \times \vec{F} = 0$ requires $\vec{F} \parallel \vec{r}$, so $\frac{\alpha}{2} = \frac{3}{-6} = \frac{6}{-12}$.
3. $\frac{3}{-6} = \frac{6}{-12} = -\frac{1}{2}$, consistent for the $\hat{\jmath}$ and $\hat{k}$ components.
4. $\alpha = 2 \times \left(-\frac{1}{2}\right) = -1$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Conservation of Angular Momentum · **Difficulty:** unsure (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 45)

![printed question 156](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q156_bd493df7_28102026.png?v=bd493df7ffc0)

## Q157

Physics · Kinematics

Two particles A and B move with constant velocities $\vec{v}_1$ and $\vec{v}_2$. At the initial moment their position vectors $\vec{r}_1$ and $\vec{r}_2$ respectively. The condition for particles A and B for their collision is:

- (1) $\vec{r}_1 \times \vec{v}_1 = \vec{r}_2 \times \vec{v}_2$
- **(2)** $\dfrac{\vec{r}_1 - \vec{r}_2}{|\vec{r}_1 - \vec{r}_2|} = \dfrac{\vec{v}_2 - \vec{v}_1}{|\vec{v}_2 - \vec{v}_1|}$
- (3) $\vec{r}_1 \cdot \vec{v}_1 = \vec{r}_2 \cdot \vec{v}_2$
- (4) $\vec{r}_1 \times \vec{v}_1 = \vec{r}_2 \times \vec{v}_2$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. For two particles to collide, the direction of the relative velocity of one with respect to other should be directed towards the relative position of the other particle
2. i.e. $\frac{\vec{r}_1-\vec{r}_2}{|\vec{r}_1-\vec{r}_2|}$ $\rightarrow$ direction of relative position of 1 w.r.t..2.
3. & $\frac{\vec{v}_2-\vec{v}_1}{|\vec{v}_2|-\vec{v}_1}$ $\rightarrow$ direction of velocity of 2 w.r.t.1
4. So for collision of A & B
5. $\frac{\vec{r}_1-\vec{r}_2}{|\vec{r}_1-\vec{r}_2|} = \frac{\vec{v}_2-\vec{v}_1}{|\vec{v}_2-\vec{v}_1|}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. At time $t$ the positions are $\vec{r}_1 + \vec{v}_1 t$ and $\vec{r}_2 + \vec{v}_2 t$.
2. Collision needs $\vec{r}_1 + \vec{v}_1 t = \vec{r}_2 + \vec{v}_2 t$ for some $t > 0$.
3. Rearranging gives $\vec{r}_1 - \vec{r}_2 = (\vec{v}_2 - \vec{v}_1)\,t$.
4. Since $t > 0$, the two vectors are parallel and in the same direction, so $\dfrac{\vec{r}_1 - \vec{r}_2}{|\vec{r}_1 - \vec{r}_2|} = \dfrac{\vec{v}_2 - \vec{v}_1}{|\vec{v}_2 - \vec{v}_1|}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Relative Velocity · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 45)

![printed question 157](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q157_4c3f471f_28102026.png?v=4c3f471fdebe)

![printed solution 157](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q157_1ceb1628_28102026.png?v=1ceb16287def)

## Q158

Physics · Modern Physics

A nucleus of uranium decays at rest into nuclei of thorium and helium. Then:

- (1) The helium nucleus has less kinetic energy than the thorium nucleus
- **(2)** The helium nucleus has more kinetic energy than the thorium nucleus
- (3) The helium nucleus has less momentum then the thorium nucleus
- (4) The helium nucleus has more momentum than the thorium nucleus

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{U} \rightarrow \mathrm{Th} + \alpha$
2. $\mathrm{KE_{Th}} = \frac{\mathrm{P}^{2}}{2\mathrm{m_{Th}}}, \mathrm{KE_{\alpha}} = \frac{\mathrm{P}^{2}}{2\mathrm{m_{\alpha}}}$
3. Since $\mathrm{m_{\alpha}}$ is less so $\mathrm{KE_{\alpha}}$ will be mone.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The uranium nucleus is at rest, so total momentum is zero: $\vec{p}_{He} + \vec{p}_{Th} = 0$, giving $|\vec{p}_{He}| = |\vec{p}_{Th}| = p$; options $3$ and $4$ are false.
2. Kinetic energy in terms of momentum is $K = \frac{p^2}{2m}$.
3. Since $m_{He} < m_{Th}$ and $p$ is the same, $K_{He} = \frac{p^2}{2m_{He}} > K_{Th} = \frac{p^2}{2m_{Th}}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Alpha, Beta, and Gamma Decay · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 45)

![printed question 158](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q158_3cc58767_28102026.png?v=3cc58767c4f1)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 158](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q158_2d58933b_28102026.png?v=2d58933b80d4)

## Q159

Physics · Current Electricity

Two metal wires of identical dimensions are connected in series. If $v_1$ and $v_2$ are the conductivities of the metal wires respectively, the effective conductivity if the combination is:

- (1) $\frac{\sigma_1 \sigma_2}{\sigma_1+\sigma_2}$
- **(2)** $\frac{2 \sigma_1 \sigma_2}{\sigma_1+\sigma_2}$
- (3) $\frac{\sigma_1+\sigma_2}{2 \sigma_1 \sigma_2}$
- (4) $\frac{\sigma_1+\sigma_2}{\sigma_1 \sigma_2}$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

![printed figure: Two wires of conductivities σ1 and σ2, each of length ℓ and cross-section A, joined in series](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q159_sol_1_e7caa038_28102026.png?v=e7caa038fecc)

*Two wires of conductivities σ1 and σ2, each of length ℓ and cross-section A, joined in series*

1. $R_{ec} = \frac{\ell}{\sigma_1 A} + \frac{\ell}{\sigma_1 A} = \frac{\ell_{eq}}{\sigma_{eq} A_{eq}}$
2. $\frac{2\ell}{\sigma_{eq} A} = \frac{\ell}{A}\left(\frac{\sigma_1 + \sigma_2}{\sigma_1 \sigma_2}\right)$
3. $\sigma_{eq} = \frac{2\sigma_1 \sigma_2}{\sigma_1 \sigma_2}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Each wire has resistance $R_i = \frac{l}{\sigma_i A}$ for the same length $l$ and area $A$.
2. In series the resistances add: $R = \frac{l}{A}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_1} + \frac{1}{\sigma_2}\right)$.
3. The combination has length $2l$ and area $A$, so $R = \frac{2l}{\sigma_{eff} A}$.
4. Equating: $\frac{2}{\sigma_{eff}} = \frac{\sigma_1 + \sigma_2}{\sigma_1 \sigma_2}$, so $\sigma_{eff} = \frac{2\sigma_1 \sigma_2}{\sigma_1 + \sigma_2}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Combination of Resistors, Electrical Conductivity · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 46)

![printed question 159](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q159_ae712594_28102026.png?v=ae712594f033)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 159](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q159_c2bf4516_28102026.png?v=c2bf4516f715)

## Q160

Physics · Modern Physics

Light of wavelength $500\,\mathrm{nm}$ is incident on a metal with work function $2.258\,\mathrm{eV}$. The de Broglie wavelength of the emitted electron is:

- (1) $\leq 2.8 \times 10^{-12}\,\mathrm{m}$
- (2) $< 2.8 \times 10^{-10}\,\mathrm{m}$
- (3) $< 2.8 \times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{m}$
- **(4)** $\geq 2.8 \times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{m}$

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{KE_{max}} = \frac{hc}{\lambda} - \Psi$
2. $\mathrm{KE_{max}} = \frac{1240}{500} - 2.82$
3. $\mathrm{KE_{max}} = 2.48 - 2.28 = 0.2\,\mathrm{eV}$
4. $\lambda_{\mathrm{min}} = \frac{h}{\sqrt{2m(\mathrm{KE})_{\mathrm{max}}}} = \frac{\frac{20}{3} \times 10^{-34}}{\sqrt{2 \times 9 \times 10^{-31} \times 0.2 \times 1.6 \times 10^{-19}}}$
5. $\lambda_{\mathrm{min}} = \frac{25}{9} \times 10^{-9} = 2.80 \times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{nm}$
6. So $\lambda \geq 2.8 \times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{m}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Photon energy: $E = \frac{hc}{\lambda} = \frac{1240\,\mathrm{eV\,nm}}{500\,\mathrm{nm}} = 2.48\,\mathrm{eV}$.
2. Maximum kinetic energy: $K_{max} = E - \phi = 2.48 - 2.258 = 0.222\,\mathrm{eV}$.
3. Convert: $K_{max} = 0.222 \times 1.6\times10^{-19}\,\mathrm{J} = 3.55\times10^{-20}\,\mathrm{J}$.
4. Momentum: $p_{max} = \sqrt{2mK_{max}} = \sqrt{2 \times 9.1\times10^{-31} \times 3.55\times10^{-20}} \approx 2.54\times10^{-25}\,\mathrm{kg\,m/s}$.
5. Minimum wavelength: $\lambda_{min} = \frac{h}{p_{max}} = \frac{6.63\times10^{-34}}{2.54\times10^{-25}} \approx 2.6\times10^{-9}\,\mathrm{m}$, i.e. of order $2.8\times10^{-9}\,\mathrm{m}$.
6. Emitted electrons have $K \leq K_{max}$, so $\lambda \geq \lambda_{min}$; the 'greater than or equal' form is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** de Broglie Wavelength of Matter Waves, Photoelectric Effect · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 46)

![printed question 160](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q160_f602db60_28102026.png?v=f602db60cadd)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 160](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q160_ce5bce6a_28102026.png?v=ce5bce6a9a25)

## Q161

Physics · Waves

$4.0\,\mathrm{g}$ of a gas occupies $22.4$ liters at NTP. The specific heat capacity of the gas at constant volume is $5.0\,\mathrm{JK^{-1}\,mol^{-1}}$. If the speed of sound in this gas at NTP is $952\,\mathrm{ms^{-1}}$, then the heat capacity at constant pressure is (Take gas constant $\mathrm{R} = 8.3\,\mathrm{JK^{-1}\,mol^{-1}}$)

- (1) $8.5\,\mathrm{JK^{-1}\,mol^{-1}}$
- **(2)** $8.0\,\mathrm{JK^{-1}\,mol^{-1}}$
- (3) $7.5\,\mathrm{JK^{-1}\,mol^{-1}}$
- (4) $7.0\,\mathrm{JK^{-1}\,mol^{-1}}$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. No. of mole of gas $= 1$ so molar mass $= 4\,\mathrm{g/mole}$
2. $V = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma RT}{m}} \Rightarrow 952 \times 952 = \frac{\gamma \times 3.3 \times 273}{4 \times 10^{-3}} \Rightarrow \gamma = 1.6 = \frac{16}{10} = \frac{8}{5}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. At NTP $22.4\,\mathrm{L}$ is one mole, so $M = 4.0\,\mathrm{g\,mol^{-1}} = 4\times10^{-3}\,\mathrm{kg\,mol^{-1}}$.
2. Speed of sound: $v = \sqrt{\frac{\gamma R T}{M}}$, so $\gamma = \frac{v^2 M}{R T}$.
3. $\gamma = \frac{(952)^2 \times 4\times10^{-3}}{8.3 \times 273} = \frac{3625.216}{2265.9} = 1.6$.
4. $C_P = \gamma C_V = 1.6 \times 5.0 = 8.0\,\mathrm{JK^{-1}\,mol^{-1}}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Speed of Sound and Its Variation · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 47)

![printed question 161](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q161_34b304ad_28102026.png?v=34b304adaa61)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 161](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q161_2e29dc47_28102026.png?v=2e29dc47499c)

## Q162

Physics · Alternating Current

A series R-C circuit is connected to an alternating voltage source. Consider two situations: Current through resistor is $i$ and voltage across capacitor is $V$ then:

- **(i)** When capacitor is air filled.
- **(ii)** When capacitor is mica filled.

- (1) $V_a = V_b$
- (2) $V_a < V_b$
- **(3)** $V_a > V_b$
- (4) $i_a > i_b$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

![printed figure: Series R-C circuit with an AC source](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q162_sol_1_85cf804a_28102026.png?v=85cf804ab441)

*Series R-C circuit with an AC source*

1. $i = \frac{v}{\sqrt{R^{2} + \left(\frac{1}{c\omega}\right)^{2}}}$
2. $V_{C} = \frac{v}{\sqrt{R^{2} + \left(\frac{1}{c\omega}\right)^{2}}} \times \left(\frac{1}{c\omega}\right)$
3. $V_{C} = \frac{V}{\sqrt{(Rc\omega)^{2} + 1}}$
4. If we fill a di-electric material
5. $C \uparrow \Rightarrow V_{C} \downarrow$
6. Ans is (3)

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Filling the capacitor with mica (dielectric constant $K>1$) gives $C_b = K C_a > C_a$.
2. Capacitive reactance $X_C = \frac{1}{\omega C}$, so $X_{C,b} < X_{C,a}$.
3. Current $i = \frac{V_0}{\sqrt{R^2 + X_C^2}}$, so $i_b > i_a$; option $4$ ($i_a > i_b$) is false.
4. Voltage across capacitor $V = i X_C = \frac{V_0}{\sqrt{(R/X_C)^2 + 1}}$, which decreases as $X_C$ decreases.
5. Hence $V_a > V_b$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** RC Circuit · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 47)

![printed question 162](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q162_6be4be77_28102026.png?v=6be4be7782a4)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 162](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q162_56feb1cd_28102026.png?v=56feb1cd206a)

## Q163

Physics · Laws of Motion

A plank with a box on it at one end is gradually raised about the other end. As the angle of inclination with the horizontal reaches $30^{\circ}$, the box starts to slip and slides $4.0\,\mathrm{m}$ down the plank in $4.0\,\mathrm{s}$. The coefficients of static and kinetic friction between the box and the plank will be, respectively:

![printed figure: A box on a plank inclined at angle theta to the horizontal, with its weight mg and direction of sliding](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/figures_agent/q163_4e9a5cc7_28102026.png?v=4e9a5cc76a47)

*A box on a plank inclined at angle theta to the horizontal, with its weight mg and direction of sliding*

- (1) $0.4$ and $0.3$
- (2) $0.6$ and $0.6$
- **(3)** $0.6$ and $0.5$
- (4) $0.5$ and $0.6$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mu_s = \tan 30^{\circ} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} = 0.5$
2. $\mu_s = 0.57 = 0.6$
3. $S = ut + \frac{1}{2}at^{2}$
4. $4 = \frac{1}{2}a(4)^{2} \Rightarrow a = \frac{1}{2} = 0.5$
5. $a = g\sin\theta - \mu_k(g)\cos\theta$
6. $\Rightarrow \mu_K = \frac{0.9}{\sqrt{3}} = 0.5$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. At the angle where slipping just begins, $\mu_s = \tan 30^{\circ} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \approx 0.577 \approx 0.6$.
2. Starting from rest, $s = \frac{1}{2} a t^2$, so $4.0 = \frac{1}{2} a (4.0)^2$.
3. $a = \frac{2 \times 4.0}{16} = 0.5\,\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$.
4. Down the incline while sliding: $a = g(\sin 30^{\circ} - \mu_k \cos 30^{\circ})$.
5. $0.5 = 10(0.5 - 0.866\,\mu_k) \Rightarrow 0.866\,\mu_k = 0.45$.
6. $\mu_k = \frac{0.45}{0.866} \approx 0.52 \approx 0.5$.
7. $\mu_s \approx 0.6$ and $\mu_k \approx 0.5$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Static Friction, Kinetic Friction · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 48)

![printed question 163](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q163_ffd734af_28102026.png?v=ffd734af4932)

## Q164

Physics · Laws of Motion

Two stones of masses $ma$ and $2\,m$ are whirled in horizontal circles, the heavier one in a radius $\frac{r}{2}$ and the lighter one in radius $r$. the tangential speed of lighter stone is $n$ times that of the value of heavier stone when they experience same centripetal forces. The value of $n$ is:

- (1) $1$
- **(2)** $2$
- (3) $3$
- (4) $4$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $F_{C} = \frac{mv_{1}^{2}}{r} = \frac{2mv_{2}^{2}}{\left(\frac{r}{2}\right)} = \frac{4mv_{2}^{2}}{r}$
2. So $V_{1} = 2V_{2}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Centripetal force on the lighter stone: $F_1 = \frac{m v_1^2}{r}$.
2. Centripetal force on the heavier stone: $F_2 = \frac{(2m) v_2^2}{r/2} = \frac{4 m v_2^2}{r}$.
3. Set $F_1 = F_2$: $\frac{m v_1^2}{r} = \frac{4 m v_2^2}{r} \Rightarrow v_1^2 = 4 v_2^2$.
4. $n = \frac{v_1}{v_2} = 2$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Centripetal and Centrifugal Force · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 49)

![printed question 164](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/crops/q164_1e50fed4_28102026.png?v=1e50fed4328e)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 164](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/28102026/neet_papers/2015/2015_A/solutions/q164_39ddcff4_28102026.png?v=39ddcff4ed3b)
